  - "Human diffusion-MRI tractography now sits on its own reading route as an acquisition-, endpoint-, graph-construction-, and calibration-conditioned macro pathway prior or targeted bundle-hypothesis route; endpoint bias, bundle priors, hub instability, and protocol dependence still have to be separated before a `connectome` paper is read strongly."
  - "Destructive human ultrastructure and living-human in vivo proxy routes strengthen different observability layers and should not be compressed into one progress bar."
  - "Living-human in vivo routes must be read on three axes at once only after route family is fixed: regional synaptic-density PET, 1H-MRSI similarity, high-resolution 1H-MRSI metabolite-distribution mapping, 31P metabolite / pH balance, 31P MT exchange-flux, 31P NAD-content mapping, localized functional 31P NAD-dynamics, deuterium metabolite-mapping / absolute quantification, deuterium kinetic-rate imaging, tract-speed estimation, MWF-versus-calibrated T1w/T2w comparison, relaxometry / MT-family comparison, bilayer-sensitive mapping, developmental diffusion-microstructure with ex vivo oligodendrocyte alignment, qT1 remyelination-sensitive pathology, T1w/FLAIR tissue-health ceilings, BBB water-exchange MRI, tracer-specific BBB PET transport, blood-CSF barrier / choroid-plexus perfusion and blood-to-CSF transport, target-defined astrocyte PET, macroscopic CSF oscillation, parenchyma-CSF water exchange, respiration-conditioned CSF net flow, exercise-conditioned contrast influx / meningeal-lymphatic flow, intrathecal tracer retention / CSF-to-blood clearance, human CSF-mobility MRI, and model-based biomarker efflux do not constrain one common object."
  - "Regional synaptic-density PET is not one route family either: tracer / quantification route, healthy atlas construction, disease contrast, task / cognition association, activation null, and intervention response remain separate reading slices before any current-state language is allowed."
  - "Same-brain scaffold papers and regional SV2A proxies do not close presynaptic release machinery: release-site number, docked-vesicle architecture, active-zone nanostructure / priming-site assembly, and current release competence remain separate audit objects."
  - "The maintenance-state causal frontier and the living-human observability frontier are different inferential ladders, so year order and citation-year conventions must not collapse them into one 2025-2026 progress bar."
  - "Multimodal papers are not one route either: synchronization infrastructure, shared-vs-specific component evidence, hemodynamic / metabolic quantity bridge, and bundle robustness under missing-modality or cross-centre disagreement remain separate reading objects."
  - "Neurovascular / BBB / pericyte papers do not constrain one common current-state object: pericyte controller causality, activity-linked BBB modulation, capillary-diameter control, human BBB permeability imaging, and human blood-CSF barrier / choroid-plexus transport remain separate routes."
  - "Same-subject or same-brain papers can still be bridge-limited evidence; preservation route, elapsed time, regime continuity, and registration validation remain separate audit items."
  - "Same-brain functional connectomics is now read here as a sequential local scaffold or task-bounded conditional predictor; morphology-linked label transfer, current synaptic-state evidence, and dynamical identifiability remain separate questions."
  - "Invasive BCI papers are no longer treated as one closed-loop progress bar: communication throughput / expressivity, decoder initialization, fixed-decoder durability, and adaptive stabilization / recalibration are separate operational routes."
  - "Closed-loop papers are not promoted from latency or drift metrics alone; slow internal-milieu routes such as circadian phase, glucocorticoid state, and insulin / metabolic regime remain separate disclosure burdens."
  - "Neuromodulatory papers do not constrain one common inferential object: mixed arousal proxy, local transmitter sensing, receptor / transporter atlas prior, occupancy PET, and challenge-linked displacement PET stay separate reading routes."
  - "A paper about m6A does not by itself fix one universal hippocampal reader assignment or the current whole-brain post-transcriptional RNA controller state."
  - "Recent maintenance-state papers now split across intrinsic-excitability control, transcriptional stabilization, post-transcriptional RNA control, phospho-signaling, local proteostasis, cargo-routing, ECM / PNN gate state, ionic / chloride regulation, bioenergetic / mitochondrial support, neurovascular-unit / BBB / pericyte support, clearance / immune support, and astrocyte-state evidence, so they should not be filed only as generic background on support variables."
  - "Phospho-signaling papers also need an explicit route-card split here: phosphosite-specific plasticity gates, learning-linked local phosphorylation, compartmentalized second-messenger routing, circadian phospho-timing gates, region-specific synapse relocalization, phospho-mutant interventions, and human ex vivo atlas ceilings do not constrain the same inferential object."
  - "Shared extracellular / electrical-state papers do not constrain one common object either: gap-junction coupling, ephaptic field effects, extracellular-space geometry / diffusion barriers / osmotic regime, sleep-linked interstitial-space shifts, and bounded human diffusion-MRI or perturbation clues remain separate routes."
  - "Thermodynamic / irreversibility papers are now treated here as route-family claims: coarse-grained lower bounds, time-asymmetry classifiers / graph measures, model-based entropy-flow estimates, and physiology-side grounding are not one common measurement."

  - "Invasive BCI papers are now read on four operational routes: communication speed / expressivity, decoder initialization, fixed-decoder durability, and adaptive stabilization / recalibration."
  - "EEG foundation-model papers are now treated as a distinct technical route because corpus disclosure, adaptation regime, benchmark governance, and leaderboard postmortems materially change what a score means."
  - "ESI / source-imaging papers are now paired with a field-formation visibility route, because visibility failure is upstream of inverse-solver disagreement and direct validation."
  - "Inverse-family source-imaging papers are now read on separate routes as well: focal posterior support, sparse debiased inference, extended-source uncertainty, forward-model / conductivity sensitivity, and named validation boards are not one generic `better ESI` line."
  - "Wearable OPM-MEG is now read as a route-conditioned movement-tolerant measurement extension: shielding class, field control, calibration / coregistration, anatomy route, crosstalk, and task regime are not collapsed into one `portable MEG` label."
  - "Human tractography papers are now read as route-conditioned macro pathway priors or targeted bundle-hypothesis routes, because endpoint access, anatomical priors, graph construction, protocol choice, and external calibration remain separate burdens."
  - "Recent human measurement papers are treated here as two linked but distinct classes: destructive local structure with explicit preservation / registration / proofreading burden, and living-human in vivo proxy routes whose route family has to be fixed before proxy class, operational maturity, and safe calibrator role are read."
  - "Human myelin / oligodendrocyte timing evidence is now split at the front door as tract-speed estimation, MWF-versus-calibrated T1w/T2w comparison, relaxometry / MT-family comparison, bilayer-sensitive mapping, developmental diffusion-microstructure with ex vivo oligodendrocyte alignment, qT1 remyelination-sensitive pathology, and T1w/FLAIR tissue-health ceiling rather than one generic `myelin MRI` row."
  - "Regional synaptic-density PET is not one route even inside the living-human lane: tracer / quantification route, healthy atlas construction, disease contrast, task / cognition association, activation null, and intervention response answer different questions."
  - "Same-brain scaffold papers and regional SV2A proxies are now stopped explicitly below presynaptic release machinery, because release-site number, docked-vesicle architecture, active-zone nanostructure / priming-site assembly, and current release competence remain separate claim ceilings."
  - "Same-brain functional connectomics is no longer compressed into one `local twin` route: sequential bridge, label source, current synaptic-state ceiling, and dynamical degeneracy are kept separate."
  - "Multimodal papers are now read here on four separate routes: synchronized acquisition, shared-vs-specific component evidence, quantity bridge / physiology grounding, and bundle robustness under availability or centre-transfer stress."
  - "Same-subject or same-brain evidence is now treated as a separate bridge problem, because sequential acquisition, preservation change, and cross-day regime drift do not collapse into one state sample."
  - "Closed-loop / deployment papers are now blocked from overread unless fast timing, drift burden, and slow internal-milieu disclosure are all visible."
  - "Neuromodulatory papers are now treated as a distinct route-family split because mixed proxies, local sensors, atlas priors, occupancy PET, and displacement PET answer different questions."
  - "Neurovascular / BBB / pericyte papers are now treated as a distinct route-family split because pericyte support causality, activity-linked BBB modulation, capillary-diameter control, human BBB permeability routes, and human blood-CSF barrier / choroid-plexus transport routes answer different questions."
  - "Shared extracellular / electrical-state papers are now treated as a distinct route-family split because gap-junction coupling, ephaptic fields, extracellular-space geometry / osmotic regime, and bounded human diffusion-MRI or perturbation clues do not share one claim ceiling."
  - "Post-transcriptional RNA evidence is no longer one maintenance row at the literature front door: splice-isoform control, transsynaptic splice-dependent receptor balance, m6A translation, dentate-gyrus- versus forebrain-scale YTHDF2-mediated decay routes, RNA editing, and atlas ceilings now stay separate."
  - "Phospho-signaling is no longer one maintenance row at the literature front door: phosphosite-specific plasticity gates, learning-linked local phosphorylation, compartmentalized second-messenger routing, circadian phospho-timing gates, region-specific synapse relocalization, phospho-mutant interventions, and human ex vivo atlas ceilings now stay separate."
  - "Recent maintenance-state papers are treated here as route-changing family-split evidence because intrinsic-excitability control, transcriptional stabilization, post-transcriptional RNA control, phospho-signaling, local proteostasis, cargo-routing, ECM / PNN gate state, ionic / chloride regulation, bioenergetic / mitochondrial support, neurovascular-unit / BBB / pericyte support, clearance / immune support, and astrocyte multiday traces remain outside connectome-only reading."
  - "Recent thermodynamic papers are treated here as an auxiliary physical-grounding route only after route family, coarse-graining / timescale, and physiology-side grounding are disclosed."

<header>
<h1>Mind Uploading Paper Collection</h1>
<p>This is an archive of major "Mind Uploading" related papers from the past 10 years, organized by the presence or absence of abstracts. First of all, we assume that you will be able to look at the flow and summaries by year, and then go back to the original documents for only the necessary papers. </p>
<div class="stats-grid">
<div class="stat-card"><span>Total</span>
<div>100</div>
</div>
<div class="stat-card"><span>With Abstract</span>
<div>47</div>
</div>
<div class="stat-card"><span>No Abstract</span>
<div>53</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="key-points">
<h4>Before reading</h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>The role of this page:</strong>To create an entry point for related literature. This is not the only page where you can complete the final evaluation of each paper. </li>
<li><strong>Where to look first:</strong>First, check the statistics and yearly order, then carefully read only the papers that are closest to your interests. </li>
<li><strong>Points to be aware of:</strong>Since papers that are widely related are also included, inclusion does not necessarily mean core evidence. </li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Reading order when lost</strong>
<p>
First, check the overall volume using the statistics above, then read the summaries of just a few books from the latest year to get a sense of the trends in the issues. If you want to know more about the position of individual papers, you can return to <a href="research_harvest_50.html">Bibliography Map</a> and check the correspondence with unresolved issues.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>When you want to know the position of this page first</strong>
<p>
This collection of papers is a "wide-ranging entry point," and the <a href="research_harvest_50.html">Bibliography Map</a> is a "place to organize by unresolved problems." If you're unsure about the difference, looking at <a href="wiki/literature-and-evidence-reading.html">Wiki: How to read literature and evidence pages</a> first will help you decide how deep to read.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>When you want to distinguish between scenes where you can stop with a summary and scenes where you can return to the original text</strong>
<p>
This page is your gateway to a broader view of the paper, so there is no need to carefully read the entire text from the beginning. On the other hand, when using important papers as evidence, you need to go back to the DOI and Original Abstract. If you want to see the different uses in one page, please see <a href="wiki/summary-vs-source-reading.html">Wiki: How to use summary, original text, and issue history</a>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>When you want to decide on the next page after reading a paper</strong>
<p>
If you find a paper in this collection of papers and want to know whether to go back to unresolved problems or link it to a proposal or issue, please see <a href="wiki/literature-to-action-route.html">Wiki: Straight path from literature to implementation/participation</a>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>When the Scopus or arXiv display stops</strong>
<p>
Badges are a clue as to where the document can be traced, but they alone do not determine the strength of the evidence. If you want to check the differences between Scopus, arXiv, Review, Media, source_logged, and evidence classes such as direct validation / system demo / observability-class advance / standard / benchmark / context in one page, it is safe to read <a href="wiki/paper-source-types-and-evidence-status.html">Wiki: How to read source types, status labels, and evidence classes</a> first.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>When you want to read only technology/natural science first</strong>
<p>
This collection of papers is a <strong>broad archive</strong>, so the first year of 2025 is not necessarily in order of technological frontier. In fact, law, metaphysics, and VR work theory are also arranged in the same year. Therefore, if you want to follow the primary evidence in technology and natural sciences, instead of reading from the top in chronological order, first enter from the <a href="#technical-fast-lane">preferred route for technology and natural sciences</a> below.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Technical chronology policy for the 2025-2026 frontier</strong>
<p>
On the core technical pages, this site cites papers by the <strong>issue year used in the journal record</strong>, but it does not let raw year adjacency define one frontier step. The reason is concrete. Nature lists <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09774-6" target="_blank">Terceros et al.</a> as <strong>Published: 26 November 2025</strong> while the citation line is <strong>Nature volume 649, pages 1254-1263 (2026)</strong>. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09619-2" target="_blank">Dewa et al.</a> is <strong>Published: 15 October 2025</strong> with <strong>issue date 04 December 2025</strong>. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02073-3" target="_blank">Hirschler et al.</a> is <strong>Published: 14 October 2025</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-68374-8" target="_blank">Dagum et al.</a> is <strong>Published: 27 January 2026</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-10068-0" target="_blank">Bukalo et al.</a> is <strong>Published: 11 February 2026</strong>. Therefore, a <code>2025-2026</code> block is not one homogeneous technical frontier. Before the year cards influence your reading, first name whether the paper advances <strong>local causal maintenance-state dependence</strong> or <strong>bounded living-human observability</strong>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>If you want the 2026 technical addendum first</strong>
<p>
This refresh adds eight explicit corrections. First, human measurement papers now have to be read on <strong>three axes</strong> only after their <strong>route family</strong> is fixed: <strong>which proxy class they constrain</strong>, <strong>how specialized or model-heavy the route still is</strong>, and <strong>what bounded calibrator role the route can safely play</strong>. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-66124-w" target="_blank">Lucchetti et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>five-metabolite similarity scaffold</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/nbm.3384" target="_blank">Ren et al. (2015)</a> constrains <strong>resting 31P metabolite / pH balance</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.26560" target="_blank">Ren et al. (2017)</a> constrains a <strong>31P MT exchange-flux route</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fnins.2024.1389111" target="_blank">Guo et al. (2024)</a> constrains a <strong>whole-brain intracellular NAD-content map</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X261415784" target="_blank">Kaiser et al. (2026)</a> constrains <strong>functionally localized 31P NAD<sup>+</sup> dynamics</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.70308" target="_blank">Karkouri et al. (2026)</a> constrains <strong>absolute deuterated metabolite distributions</strong> under an explicit quantification pipeline; and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/pnasnexus/pgaf072" target="_blank">Li et al. (2025)</a> adds <strong>kinetic glucose-rate imaging</strong> by dynamic DMRSI. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/jmri.29532" target="_blank">Ahmadian et al. (2025)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1186/s41747-024-00426-4" target="_blank">Bøgh et al. (2024)</a> further show that <strong>dose choice</strong> and <strong>repeatability</strong> are route-card burdens for deuterium work rather than generic portability guarantees. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/nbm.5256" target="_blank">Morgan et al. (2024)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58356-7" target="_blank">Chung et al. (2025)</a> separate <strong>BBB water-exchange</strong> from <strong>tracer-specific BBB transport</strong>. Astrocyte PET is now split inside the tracer family as well: <a href="https://doi.org/10.2967/jnumed.121.263254" target="_blank">Villemagne et al. (2022)</a> is the <strong>first-in-human SMBT-1 MAO-B target-validation route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.2967/jnumed.121.263255" target="_blank">Villemagne et al. (2022)</a> is the <strong>AD-spectrum disease-context route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.2967/jnumed.118.208009" target="_blank">Tyacke et al. (2018)</a> separates <strong>MAO-B</strong> from <strong>I2BS</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12149-025-02083-y" target="_blank">Hiraoka et al. (2025)</a> show that the SMBT-1 MAO-B brain route still needs a named <strong>scan-window / reference-region or kinetic-model choice</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12149-025-02144-2" target="_blank">Mesfin et al. (2026)</a> add a <strong>whole-body biodistribution route</strong> in six healthy participants over a 5.5-hour dynamic protocol, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00259-025-07542-2" target="_blank">Matsuoka et al. (2026)</a> add a separate <strong>simplified / arterial-free SL25.1188 AD quantification route</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41380-025-03355-9" target="_blank">Best et al. (2026)</a> show that SL25.1188 MAO-B binding in AUD was not elevated overall and shifted with severity and daily cigarette use. The human clearance papers themselves also split: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aax5440" target="_blank">Fultz et al. (2019)</a> add <strong>macroscopic CSF oscillation</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2025.121142" target="_blank">Kim, Huang, &amp; Liu (2025)</a> add <strong>parenchyma-CSF water exchange</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-37685-5" target="_blank">Eide et al. (2023)</a> add <strong>intrathecal-tracer / CSF-to-blood-clearance-capacity evidence</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02073-3" target="_blank">Hirschler et al. (2025)</a> add <strong>CSF-mobility mapping</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-68374-8" target="_blank">Dagum et al. (2026)</a> add <strong>model-based overnight biomarker efflux</strong> with an investigational wearable plus a compartment model. Those are not one inferential object or one route burden. The same no-compression rule now applies inside the <strong>human intrinsic-excitability lane</strong>: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-025-21967-7" target="_blank">Tallman et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>human hippocampal single-unit allocation-linked route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/cercor/bhs014" target="_blank">Huber et al. (2013)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/ncomms12455" target="_blank">Kuhn et al. (2016)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2026.121723" target="_blank">Fehér et al. (2026)</a> are <strong>sleep-history / plasticity-recalibration proxies</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brs.2017.11.016" target="_blank">Zrenner et al. (2018)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brs.2024.12.1193" target="_blank">Khatri et al. (2025)</a> are <strong>state-gated perturbation proxies</strong>. Those papers do not constrain one common current excitability meter, and they still do not identify <strong>AIS geometry</strong>, <strong>ion-channel distribution</strong>, or the responsible <strong>homeostatic controller</strong> in vivo. Second, same-subject or same-brain papers now have to pass a <strong>bridge audit</strong>: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.crmeth.2023.100520" target="_blank">Lu et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-022-30199-6" target="_blank">Bosch et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08790-w" target="_blank">MICrONS Consortium et al. (2025)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-70609-x" target="_blank">Egger et al. (2024)</a> together show that preservation route, acquisition order, bridge validation, and cross-day regime drift materially change what those labels are allowed to mean. Third, the maintenance-state route now has to be read as a <strong>family split</strong> rather than as one support bucket: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-66975-3" target="_blank">Hadzibegovic et al. (2025)</a> sharpens <strong>intrinsic-excitability control</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cub.2025.01.050" target="_blank">Alfonsa et al. (2025)</a> sharpens <strong>chloride-dependent plasticity gating</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09774-6" target="_blank">Terceros et al. (2026)</a> sharpens <strong>transcriptional stabilization</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42255-026-01451-w" target="_blank">Vishwanath et al. (2026)</a> sharpens <strong>bioenergetic / mitochondrial control</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cell.2025.02.022" target="_blank">Kim et al. (2025)</a> sharpens <strong>clearance / immune support</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09619-2" target="_blank">Dewa et al. (2025)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-10068-0" target="_blank">Bukalo et al. (2026)</a> sharpen <strong>astrocyte-state</strong>. Fourth, the literature front door now also carries an <strong>ECM / PNN route-family split</strong>: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1072699" target="_blank">Pizzorusso et al. (2002)</a> is a <strong>plasticity-window reopening route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.2338" target="_blank">Frischknecht et al. (2009)</a> is a <strong>receptor-mobility / short-term-plasticity constraint route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cell.2020.05.050" target="_blank">Nguyen et al. (2020)</a> is a <strong>microglia-driven ECM-remodeling and remote-memory-precision route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matbio.2024.11.001" target="_blank">Jabłońska et al. (2024)</a> is a <strong>synapse-specific inhibitory-plasticity route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1626-24.2024" target="_blank">Alexander et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>cell-type-specific CA2-versus-PV memory-support route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/acel.70139" target="_blank">Mehak et al. (2025)</a> is an <strong>age-linked rescue route</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/epi4.12963" target="_blank">Lehner et al. (2024)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12035-024-04306-1" target="_blank">Banovac et al. (2025)</a> remain <strong>human ex vivo histology routes</strong>. Fifth, EEG foundation-model papers now have to be paired with <strong>benchmark-governance evidence</strong>: <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fnhum.2021.653659" target="_blank">Kostas et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/47393e8594c82ce8fd83adc672cf9872-Abstract-Conference.html" target="_blank">Jiang et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://openreview.net/forum?id=J5SbLoq7Uv" target="_blank">Lee et al. (2025)</a>, the official <a href="https://eeg2025.github.io/leaderboard/" target="_blank">EEG Challenge 2025 leaderboard</a>, <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17742" target="_blank">Xiong et al. (2025)</a>, and <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02268" target="_blank">Lahiri et al. (2026)</a> together show that corpus disclosure, split construction, inference-stage rules, and benchmark inconsistency can move what a leaderboard score actually means. Sixth, invasive BCI papers now have to be read as <strong>four operational routes</strong> rather than one `closed-loop success` bucket: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-023-06377-x" target="_blank">Willett et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-01905-6" target="_blank">Littlejohn et al. (2025)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09127-3" target="_blank">Wairagkar et al. (2025)</a> push <strong>communication throughput / expressivity</strong>; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1056/NEJMoa2314132" target="_blank">Card et al. (2024)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-63825-0" target="_blank">Singh et al. (2025)</a> strengthen <strong>decoder initialization</strong> in different ways, one through rapid same-subject calibration and the other through cross-subject transfer-assisted initialization; <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-024-06784-4" target="_blank">Pun et al. (2024)</a> plus the fixed-decoder slice in <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09127-3" target="_blank">Wairagkar et al. (2025)</a> expose <strong>fixed-decoder durability</strong> and drift as their own audit object; and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-59652-y" target="_blank">Karpowicz et al. (2025)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41551-025-01536-z" target="_blank">Wilson et al. (2025)</a> show that alignment-based stabilization and unsupervised recalibration are <strong>adaptive rescue routes</strong> rather than evidence that the original decoder stayed valid. Seventh, neurovascular papers now have to be read as a <strong>route-family split</strong>: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2010.09.043" target="_blank">Bell et al. (2010)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fncel.2020.00027" target="_blank">Kisler et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2023.08.030" target="_blank">Pandey et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.89611" target="_blank">Swissa et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-61312-0" target="_blank">Mai-Morente et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neurobiolaging.2024.12.012" target="_blank">Padrela et al. (2025)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58356-7" target="_blank">Chung et al. (2025)</a> together show that pericyte controller causality, activity-linked BBB modulation, capillary support, and human BBB permeability imaging do not constrain one common current-state object. Eighth, the paper-front door itself now has to carry that <strong>human route taxonomy</strong>, because the main public pages have already stopped readers from collapsing route names, and the archive entry point should not quietly reintroduce that compression.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Neurovascular route family now explicitly includes the human blood-CSF barrier</strong>
<p>
The archive front door should no longer let readers fold all human barrier-side papers into one `BBB` bucket. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/nbm.5256" target="_blank">Morgan et al. (2024)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58356-7" target="_blank">Chung et al. (2025)</a> constrain <strong>BBB water-exchange</strong> and <strong>tracer-specific BBB transport</strong>, whereas <a href="https://doi.org/10.1186/s12987-020-00218-z" target="_blank">Zhao et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118755" target="_blank">Petitclerc et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1080/21688370.2021.1963143" target="_blank">Anderson et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X251369218" target="_blank">Wu et al. (2026)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X261429042" target="_blank">Petitclerc et al. (2026)</a> constrain a distinct <strong>blood-CSF barrier / choroid-plexus perfusion / blood-to-CSF transport</strong> route family. These papers do not share one anatomical boundary, one direct observable, or one validation ceiling, so this site now keeps them separate from both generic BBB language and downstream clearance-side routes.
</p>
</div>
<section class="section" id="technical-fast-lane">
<h2 class="section-title">2026-03 Literature audit: Priority route for technology and natural sciences</h2>
<p>
The weakness of this page is that although it is useful as a <strong>chronological archive</strong>, the ordering is weak as a <strong>front door to the experimental frontier</strong>. From a technology / natural-science perspective, the first thing we want to know is (1) how far non-invasive decoding has progressed, (2) how far invasive speech neuroprosthesis has pushed the same-session communication route, (3) whether rapid or transfer-assisted <strong>decoder initialization</strong> reduces subject-specific warm-up, (4) whether communication survives <strong>fixed-decoder horizons</strong> or only after adaptive rescue, (5) how field formation, inverse-family target object, forward-model sensitivity, and named validation boards actually split what a source-imaging paper strengthens, (6) how EEG foundation / self-supervised results change once corpus disclosure, adaptation regime, split construction, and inference-stage budget are held fixed, (7) how effective-connectivity models remain conditional on candidate model space and observation assumptions even when they scale, (8) which multimodal papers advance <strong>synchronized acquisition</strong>, <strong>shared-vs-specific structure</strong>, <strong>quantity bridge / hemodynamic grounding</strong>, or <strong>bundle robustness under missing-modality and centre-transfer stress</strong>, (9) what humans can actually observe now, at which measurement class, and with what remaining route burden, (10) when a same-subject or same-brain result is still only a <strong>sequential bridge</strong> rather than one latent-state sample, (11) which maintenance-state families, including intrinsic-excitability control, ECM / PNN gate state, ionic / chloride regulation, bioenergetic / mitochondrial support, neurovascular-unit / BBB / pericyte support, clearance / immune support, transcriptional stabilization, and astrocyte-state, still remain outside the connectome, and (12) how thermodynamic / irreversibility papers split into different estimator families instead of one common physical-grounding result. It is about how much remains, not the first display of law, philosophy, or cultural theory. Therefore, on this page, we clearly indicate the following as the <strong>preferred route</strong>.
</p>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Same-brain functional connectomics is not a solved local twin</strong>
<p>
The literature front door still needed a narrower stop rule here. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-022-30199-6" target="_blank">Bosch et al. (2022)</a> showed that live physiology to volume-EM correlation is a <strong>multistage landmark-based bridge</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08790-w" target="_blank">MICrONS Consortium et al. (2025)</a> strengthened that route into a powerful <strong>same-brain local structure-function pipeline</strong>, not a simultaneous whole-state sample. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08840-3" target="_blank">Ding et al. (2025)</a> then added a <strong>validated stimulus-conditioned response model</strong>, whereas <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08805-6" target="_blank">Gamlin et al. (2025)</a> still linked transcriptomic identity through <strong>morphology-based prediction</strong> rather than direct transcriptomic assay inside the EM volume. At the synapse and dynamics level, <a href="https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.18167" target="_blank">Moln&aacute;r et al. (2016)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-017-0041-9" target="_blank">Sakamoto et al. (2018)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-020-03134-2" target="_blank">Holler et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-022-33565-6" target="_blank">D&uuml;rst et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-024-01720-5" target="_blank">Emperador-Melero et al. (2024)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-024-53901-2" target="_blank">Mittermaier et al. (2024)</a> show that current synaptic efficacy, release-site number, active-zone nanostructure / priming-site assembly, release probability, and membrane-state-gated consolidation are not exhausted by structure-function correspondence, while <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02080-4" target="_blank">Beiran &amp; Litwin-Kumar (2025)</a> show that connectome-constrained dynamics can remain degenerate until extra recordings reduce the solution family. Therefore, this archive now reads same-brain functional connectomics as a <strong>sequential local scaffold</strong> or <strong>task-bounded conditional predictor</strong>, not as direct transcriptomic truth, presynaptic release-machinery truth, current synaptic-state readout, or a solved local twin.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Human tractography connectomes are route-conditioned macro pathway priors</strong>
<p>
The archive front door also still needed a tractography stop rule. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1405672111" target="_blank">Thomas et al. (2014)</a> showed that tractography accuracy is inherently limited when only voxel-averaged local orientation estimates are available, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/hbm.23936" target="_blank">Schilling et al. (2018)</a> showed persistent gyral-endpoint bias across deterministic and probabilistic algorithms, multiple diffusion models, and even high-resolution data, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00429-020-02129-z" target="_blank">Schilling et al. (2020)</a> showed that high anatomical accuracy appears mainly when strong start / end / exclusion priors are supplied, which is a <strong>targeted bundle-hypothesis route</strong> rather than generic edge-complete recovery. Downstream graph meaning is unstable too: <a href="https://doi.org/10.1162/netn_a_00324" target="_blank">Gajwani et al. (2023)</a> showed across <strong>1,760 group connectomes</strong> built from <strong>40 pipelines</strong> and <strong>44 group-reconstruction schemes</strong> that hub location depends strongly on processing choices, while <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.mri.2025.110424" target="_blank">McMaster et al. (2025)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fnimg.2026.1670604" target="_blank">Bramati et al. (2026)</a> show that voxel-size harmonization and diffusion-sampling scheme still move tractography outputs. Even newer gains such as <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.media.2025.103498" target="_blank">Zhu et al. (2025)</a> improve reconstruction through <strong>hybrid MRI-microscopy calibration</strong>, not by turning living-human tractography into a finished connectome. Therefore, this archive now reads a human tractography paper first as an <strong>acquisition-, endpoint-, graph-construction-, and calibration-conditioned macro pathway prior</strong> or, at best, a <strong>targeted bundle-hypothesis route</strong>. It is not read as a synapse-resolved edge list, one stable hub map, or a WBE-relevant structural completion claim. For the operating rule, go next to <a href="wiki/connectome-is-not-enough.html#tractography-route-card">Wiki: tractography route card</a> and <a href="verification.html#observability-budget">Verification: Observability Budget</a>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Shared extracellular / electrical-state papers are not one maintenance route</strong>
<p>
The literature front door still had one missing U3 split. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/47029" target="_blank">Galarreta &amp; Hestrin (1999)</a> showed a <strong>gap-junction coupling network</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.2727" target="_blank">Anastassiou et al. (2011)</a> showed <strong>endogenous-field / ephaptic spike-timing bias</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.4493-13.2014" target="_blank">Graydon et al. (2014)</a> showed <strong>local extracellular-volume-fraction geometry and transmitter dilution</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/jnr.20871" target="_blank">Kilb et al. (2006)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1241224" target="_blank">Xie et al. (2013)</a> showed that <strong>osmotic or sleep-linked extracellular-space changes</strong> can alter excitability or exchange without rewiring, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2020.116682" target="_blank">Voldsbekk et al. (2020)</a> remained a <strong>bounded human diffusion-MRI extracellular-space proxy clue</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/ejn.70401" target="_blank">Feld et al. (2026)</a> remained a <strong>human perturbation-conditioned electrical-synapse clue</strong> with pharmacological caveats. Therefore, this archive no longer lets <code>shared extracellular / electrical state</code> stand in for one common inferential object. The safe reading now has to name whether the paper advances <strong>gap-junction coupling</strong>, <strong>ephaptic field effects</strong>, <strong>extracellular-space geometry / diffusion barriers / osmotic regime</strong>, <strong>sleep-linked interstitial-space proxy</strong>, or only a <strong>bounded human clue</strong>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Read ECM / PNN papers through an explicit route-family split</strong>
<p>
The literature front door also still had one missing U3 split in the matrix lane. The primary literature does not support reading <strong>ECM / PNN evidence</strong> as one generic support variable. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1072699" target="_blank">Pizzorusso et al. (2002)</a> is a <strong>plasticity-window reopening route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.2338" target="_blank">Frischknecht et al. (2009)</a> is a <strong>receptor-mobility / short-term-plasticity constraint route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cell.2020.05.050" target="_blank">Nguyen et al. (2020)</a> is a <strong>microglia-driven ECM-remodeling and remote-memory-precision route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matbio.2024.11.001" target="_blank">Jabłońska et al. (2024)</a> is a <strong>synapse-specific inhibitory-plasticity route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1626-24.2024" target="_blank">Alexander et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>cell-type-specific CA2-versus-PV memory-support route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/acel.70139" target="_blank">Mehak et al. (2025)</a> is an <strong>age-linked rescue route</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/epi4.12963" target="_blank">Lehner et al. (2024)</a> plus <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12035-024-04306-1" target="_blank">Banovac et al. (2025)</a> remain <strong>human ex vivo histology routes</strong>. Therefore, this archive no longer lets one matrix paper stand in for <strong>current whole-brain ECM gate state</strong>, <strong>one common plasticity meter</strong>, or <strong>a solved human maintenance readout</strong>. The safe reading now has to name whether the paper advances plasticity-window reopening, receptor-mobility constraint, microglial remodeling, inhibitory-plasticity control, CA2-versus-PV memory support, age-linked rescue, or human ex vivo histology.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Read human clearance papers through an explicit route-family split</strong>
<p>
The literature front door still had one remaining compression inside the human clearance lane. The current primary literature does not support reading <strong>human clearance-support evidence</strong> as one transport row. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aax5440" target="_blank">Fultz et al. (2019)</a> is a <strong>macroscopic sleep-state CSF-oscillation route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2025.121142" target="_blank">Kim, Huang, &amp; Liu (2025)</a> is a <strong>parenchyma-CSF water-exchange route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-66548-4" target="_blank">Lim et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>respiration-conditioned net-flow route</strong> whose direct observable remains plane-specific awake-state CSF displacement, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58726-1" target="_blank">Yoo et al. (2025)</a> is an <strong>exercise-conditioned contrast-influx / meningeal-lymphatic route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-37685-5" target="_blank">Eide et al. (2023)</a> is an <strong>intrathecal-tracer / CSF-to-blood-clearance-capacity route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02073-3" target="_blank">Hirschler et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>CSF-mobility MRI route</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-68374-8" target="_blank">Dagum et al. (2026)</a> is a <strong>model-based overnight biomarker-efflux route</strong>. Therefore, this archive no longer lets one clearance paper stand in for <strong>route-free whole-brain bulk circulation</strong>, <strong>local immune-controller identity</strong>, or <strong>one solved human maintenance readout</strong>. The safe reading now has to name which transport object, intervention regime, and model burden the paper actually constrains.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Read myelin / oligodendrocyte timing papers through an explicit route-family split</strong>
<p>
The literature front door still had one remaining compression inside the human timing-support lane. The primary literature does not support reading <strong>myelin / oligodendrocyte evidence</strong> as one reusable human row. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-023-01272-0" target="_blank">van Blooijs et al. (2023)</a> is a <strong>tract-scale transmission-speed route</strong>, <a href="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5342928/" target="_blank">Arshad et al. (2017)</a> is an <strong>MWF versus calibrated T<sub>1</sub>w/T<sub>2</sub>w comparison route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-018-28852-6" target="_blank">Hagiwara et al. (2018)</a> is a <strong>relaxometry / MT<sub>sat</sub> comparison route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.29998" target="_blank">Baadsvik et al. (2024)</a> is a <strong>bilayer-sensitive proof-of-principle route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58604-w" target="_blank">Genc et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>developmental diffusion-microstructure route with ex vivo oligodendrocyte-expression alignment</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2025.121456" target="_blank">Chen et al. (2025)</a> show that <strong>orientation dependence inside MT-family imaging is itself a route burden</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/bpa.70010" target="_blank">Galbusera et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>qT1 remyelination-sensitive pathology route</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00234-025-03875-9" target="_blank">Colaes et al. (2026)</a> show that <strong>T<sub>1</sub>w/FLAIR may remain a general tissue-health marker rather than a myelin-specific readout</strong>. Therefore, this archive no longer lets <code>myelin MRI</code> stand in for one common observability class or for per-axon timing-state ground truth. The safe reading now has to name whether the paper advances tract-speed estimation, cross-metric comparison, bilayer-sensitive mapping, developmental oligodendrocyte alignment, remyelination-sensitive pathology, or only a tissue-health ceiling. For the operating rule, go next to <a href="wiki/homeostatic-plasticity-and-maintenance-state.html#myelin-route-card">Wiki: myelin / oligodendrocyte route card</a> and <a href="verification.html#human-proxy-composition-card">Verification: Human Proxy Composition Card</a>.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Read phospho-signaling papers through an explicit route-family split</strong>
<p>
The literature front door still had one missing U3 split inside the molecular-maintenance lane. The primary literature does not support reading <strong>phospho-signaling / second-messenger evidence</strong> as one generic controller row. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/S0092-8674(03)00122-3" target="_blank">Lee et al. (2003)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.5303-03.2004" target="_blank">Rodrigues et al. (2004)</a> are <strong>phosphosite-specific plasticity and learning-linked local phosphorylation routes</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.0248-16.2016" target="_blank">Havekes et al. (2016)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-40930-6" target="_blank">Vierra et al. (2023)</a> are <strong>compartmentalized second-messenger / signalosome routes</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adj1010" target="_blank">Barone et al. (2023)</a> is a <strong>circadian phospho-timing gate</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biopsych.2023.12.020" target="_blank">Altas et al. (2024)</a> is a <strong>region-specific phosphorylation and synapse-localization route</strong>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1619-24.2025" target="_blank">Rodriguez et al. (2025)</a> is a <strong>single-site phospho-mutant causal intervention</strong>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jproteome.2c00244" target="_blank">Biswas et al. (2023)</a> is a <strong>human ex vivo phosphoproteome atlas route</strong>. Therefore, this archive now asks the reader to name whether the paper advances a phosphosite-specific plasticity gate, a learning-linked local phosphorylation route, a compartmentalized second-messenger / signalosome route, a circadian phospho-timing gate, a region-specific synapse-localization route, a single-site phospho-mutant intervention, or a human ex vivo atlas ceiling before any phospho paper is promoted to generic <code>maintenance state</code> evidence.
</p>
</div>
<div class="note-box">
<strong>Closed-loop deployability is not fixed by timing and drift alone</strong>
<p>
The literature front door also needed one slower boundary. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/29542" target="_blank">de Quervain et al. (1998)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s11682-007-9003-2" target="_blank">Oei et al. (2007)</a> showed that glucocorticoid state can impair retrieval and reduce human hippocampal / prefrontal retrieval activity. <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.celrep.2020.108255" target="_blank">McCauley et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adj1010" target="_blank">Barone et al. (2023)</a>, and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2211996120" target="_blank">Birnie et al. (2023)</a> showed that circadian timing and corticosteroid rhythm change hippocampal plasticity machinery, while <a href="https://doi.org/10.3233/JAD-2008-13309" target="_blank">Reger et al. (2008)</a> and <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuropsychologia.2015.07.020" target="_blank">Sherman et al. (2015)</a> show that insulin delivery and circadian-rhythm consistency can shift human memory or hippocampal activity. Therefore, papers in the fixed-decoder, recalibration, or deployability lane are no longer read here from <strong>latency</strong>, <strong>jitter</strong>, or <strong>drift curves</strong> alone. The safe reading now also asks whether <strong>slow internal-milieu routes</strong> such as circadian phase, glucocorticoid state, and insulin / metabolic regime were controlled, measured, perturbed, or left latent.
</p>
</div>
<table class="data-table">
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Technical route to read first</th>
<th>Previous primary literature</th>
<th>What I can say now</th>
<th>Things I can't say yet</th>
<th>Next page</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><strong>Language decode / Neural Contribution Card</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-023-01304-9" target="_blank">Tang et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42256-023-00714-5" target="_blank">D&eacute;fossez et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-65499-0" target="_blank">d'Ascoli et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-025-07731-7" target="_blank">Ye et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Considerable progress has been made, but on <strong>different route types</strong>: Tang is within-subject semantic reconstruction from fMRI, D&eacute;fossez is fixed-segment speech retrieval from M/EEG, d'Ascoli is known-onset word decoding at scale, and Ye is prompt-conditioned generation with brain-derived inputs. What gets stronger directly here is <strong>neural contribution under an explicit scaffold</strong>, not one common solved brain-to-text capability.</td>
<td>It does not show unrestricted thought reading, prompt-free open-ended decoding, subject-independent stable communication, or the internal-state reconstruction required for WBE. Timing regime, candidate bank, prompt budget, and subject route still remain part of the claim.</td>
<td><a href="perspective.html#brain-to-text-update">Perspective: brain-to-text section</a> / <a href="verification.html#neural-contribution-card">Verification: Neural Contribution Card</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Invasive speech neuroprosthesis / communication throughput</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-023-06377-x" target="_blank">Willett et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-01905-6" target="_blank">Littlejohn et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09127-3" target="_blank">Wairagkar et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Achieves high-vocabulary text decoding, streaming brain-to-voice, near-instant voice synthesis, and explicit silence fallback under a task-limited communication scaffold. This strongly pushes the <strong>same-session communication subsystem</strong> frontier.</td>
<td>Whole-brain emulation, identity preservation, subject-general everyday communication, <strong>decoder initialization</strong>, <strong>fixed-decoder durability</strong>, and zero-recalibration autonomy have not yet been demonstrated.</td>
<td><a href="faq.html#brain-to-text">FAQ: how to read brain-to-text claims</a> / <a href="verification.html#neural-contribution-card">Verification: Neural Contribution Card</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Decoder initialization / transfer-assisted route</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1056/NEJMoa2314132" target="_blank">Card et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-63825-0" target="_blank">Singh et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Shows that communication systems can start from a <strong>much shorter subject-specific warm-up</strong> or from a <strong>group-derived transfer prior</strong>, reducing the amount of individualized data needed before useful decoding appears.</td>
<td>That still does not prove subject-general everyday communication, long-horizon fixed-decoder validity, robustness across coverage regimes, or low-maintenance autonomy after deployment.</td>
<td><a href="faq.html#brain-to-text">FAQ: how to read brain-to-text claims</a> / <a href="verification.html#neural-contribution-card">Verification: Neural Contribution Card</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Fixed-decoder durability / instability audit</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-024-06784-4" target="_blank">Pun et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09127-3" target="_blank">Wairagkar et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Shows that <strong>model drift</strong>, decoder-instability measures, and fixed-decoder performance decline are themselves measurable objects rather than background noise. This directly strengthens the <strong>fixed-decoder durability slice</strong>.</td>
<td>That still does not prove rescue is solved, that drift can be ignored, or that one participant and interface define a deployable stability regime.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/state-trait-and-drift.html">Wiki: state, trait, and drift</a> / <a href="wiki/closed-loop-latency-jitter-and-safety-stops.html">Wiki: closed loop, latency, jitter, and safety stops</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Adaptive stabilization / recalibration route</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-59652-y" target="_blank">Karpowicz et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41551-025-01536-z" target="_blank">Wilson et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Adaptive stabilization and unsupervised recalibration can preserve useful control across weeks to months despite accumulating neural changes. This directly strengthens the <strong>rescue route</strong>, not the claim that the original decoder stayed valid.</td>
<td>That still does not prove no drift, zero-maintenance autonomy, task-general durability, speech-specific deployability, or clinic-to-home robustness. <strong>Stabilized use</strong> and <strong>fixed-decoder validity</strong> remain different claims.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/state-trait-and-drift.html">Wiki: state, trait, and drift</a> / <a href="wiki/closed-loop-latency-jitter-and-safety-stops.html">Wiki: closed loop, latency, jitter, and safety stops</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>ESI / source imaging with direct validation</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-020-0467-x" target="_blank">Mikulan et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/braincomms/fcad023" target="_blank">Unnwongse et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/epi.18552" target="_blank">Hao et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Direct validation using known stimulus locations and simultaneous SEEG allows for significant auditing of the effects of localization error, source depth, and conductivity assumptions. </td>
<td>It is not possible to support the claim that general uniqueness restoration can be obtained even if the head model is changed. </td>
<td><a href="datasets.html#validation-ladder">Datasets: validation ladder</a> / <a href="wiki/observation-to-estimation.html">From observation to estimation</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Wearable OPM-MEG / movement-tolerant macro electrophysiology</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nature26147" target="_blank">Boto et al. (2018)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118401" target="_blank">Rea et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1109/TBME.2021.3100770" target="_blank">Mellor et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1109/TBME.2024.3465654" target="_blank">Holmes et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1162/IMAG.a.8" target="_blank">Rhodes et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0273491" target="_blank">Wu et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.3390/s25134160" target="_blank">Spedden et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Wearable OPM-MEG now supports motion-tolerant macro electrophysiology under explicitly engineered magnetic environments, including standing/mobile, lightly shielded, MRI-light source-model, and narrow whole-body-movement demonstrations. What gets stronger directly is <strong>movement-tolerant acquisition under a disclosed field-control and anatomy route</strong>, not one generic portability upgrade.</td>
<td>It still does not show shield-free ordinary-room measurement, calibration-free source truth, anatomy-free source reconstruction, broad naturalistic coverage, or a stronger claim ceiling than the EEG / MEG visibility / inverse / validation wall. Field nulling, crosstalk, and task regime remain part of the claim.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/multimodal-integration-basics.html">Wiki: multimodal integration basics</a> / <a href="wiki/measurement-stack-and-claim-ceiling.html#eeg-meg-validation-wall">Wiki: EEG / MEG validation wall</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>EEG foundation models / pretraining governance</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fnhum.2021.653659" target="_blank">Kostas et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/47393e8594c82ce8fd83adc672cf9872-Abstract-Conference.html" target="_blank">Jiang et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://openreview.net/forum?id=J5SbLoq7Uv" target="_blank">Lee et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://eeg2025.github.io/leaderboard/" target="_blank">EEG Challenge 2025</a>, <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17742" target="_blank">Xiong et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02268" target="_blank">Lahiri et al. (2026)</a></td>
<td>Large-scale pretraining clearly strengthens representation learning and low-label transfer, but the primary sources and official challenge operations also show that <strong>corpus disclosure</strong>, <strong>adaptation regime</strong>, <strong>split construction</strong>, and <strong>inference-stage budget</strong> materially change how a score should be read.</td>
<td>It does not show that generalization is solved, that larger models win by default, or that a leaderboard is already evidence of portable subject-invariant decoding, source identifiability, or WBE-relevant state capture.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/eeg-foundation-models.html">Wiki: EEG foundation models and pretraining</a> / <a href="verification.html#pretraining-card">Verification: Pretraining Card</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>effective connectivity / DCM route card</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2004.03.026" target="_blank">Penny et al. (2004)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jneumeth.2012.04.013" target="_blank">Rosa et al. (2012)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2020.117491" target="_blank">Fr&auml;ssle et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/hbm.26751" target="_blank">Zhang et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/hbm.26782" target="_blank">Jafarian et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2024.120604" target="_blank">Ma et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2024.120954" target="_blank">Wu et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/hbm.70285" target="_blank">Novelli et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>Candidate generative models can be compared at larger scale, directed-connectivity estimates can be produced faster, processing-policy sensitivity can be measured explicitly, and reliability can be quantified under tightly matched acquisition, scan-duration, and sample-size conditions. </td>
<td>A denser or faster graph is still not discovered causal wiring, and a graph that survives one preprocessing recipe or one tightly matched reliability regime is still not condition-general portability. </td>
<td><a href="wiki/observation-to-estimation.html#effective-connectivity-route-card">Wiki: effective-connectivity route card</a> / <a href="tech_roadmap.html#qa-r4">Roadmap R4</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>multimodal route split: sync / shared-factor / quantity-bridge / robustness</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1162/imag.a.136" target="_blank">Kothe et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-44363-z" target="_blank">Vafaii et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-64414-x" target="_blank">Chen et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-01945-y" target="_blank">Bolt et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02132-9" target="_blank">Epp et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/brain/awac335" target="_blank">Amiri et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/brain/awaf412" target="_blank">Manasova et al. (2026)</a></td>
<td>Primary papers now support four narrower multimodal advances: <strong>millisecond-scale synchronized acquisition</strong>, <strong>shared and divergent cross-modal structure</strong>, <strong>coupled but still quantity-bearing hemodynamic / metabolic dynamics</strong>, and sometimes <strong>bundle-performance gains under explicitly declared same-sample or multicentre regimes</strong>.</td>
<td>Shared clocks are still not device-delay truth, shared factors are still not automatically one target neural variable, BOLD-metabolic agreement is still not guaranteed, and multimodal gains can still depend on a narrow complete-case slice, missing-modality handling, or hard-regime disagreement rather than a robust common state readout.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/multimodal-integration-basics.html">Wiki: Multimodal integration basics</a> / <a href="verification.html#fusion-card">Verification: Fusion Card</a> / <a href="wiki/measurement-stack-and-claim-ceiling.html">Wiki: Observability and claim ceiling for each measurement stack</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>destructive ultrastructure / preservation-registration-proof gate</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.crmeth.2023.100520" target="_blank">Lu et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.adk4858" target="_blank">Shapson-Coe et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-024-07558-y" target="_blank">Dorkenwald et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08790-w" target="_blank">MICrONS Consortium et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>It becomes concrete that nanoscale or petascale structural papers strengthen a <strong>destructive local scaffold</strong> only after preservation route, registration scope, throughput, and proofreading burden are named explicitly.</td>
<td>Do not read these papers as live native-state capture, same-time whole-brain state measurement, or finished error-free whole-brain readiness.</td>
<td><a href="verification.html#destructive-structure-card">Verification: Destructive-Structure Route Card</a> / <a href="wiki/measurement-stack-and-claim-ceiling.html#destructive-ultrastructure-wall">Wiki: destructive ultrastructure wall</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>human in vivo observability / proxy ladder</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1750-23.2024" target="_blank">Johansen et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-66124-w" target="_blank">Lucchetti et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1109/TBME.2025.3572448" target="_blank">Guo et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/nbm.3384" target="_blank">Ren et al. (2015)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.26560" target="_blank">Ren et al. (2017)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fnins.2024.1389111" target="_blank">Guo et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X261415784" target="_blank">Kaiser et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.70308" target="_blank">Karkouri et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/pnasnexus/pgaf072" target="_blank">Li et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-023-01272-0" target="_blank">van Blooijs et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/mrm.29998" target="_blank">Baadsvik et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58604-w" target="_blank">Genc et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/bpa.70010" target="_blank">Galbusera et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00234-025-03875-9" target="_blank">Colaes et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/nbm.5256" target="_blank">Morgan et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58356-7" target="_blank">Chung et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.2967/jnumed.121.263254" target="_blank">Villemagne et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12149-025-02083-y" target="_blank">Hiraoka et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12149-025-02144-2" target="_blank">Mesfin et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00259-025-07542-2" target="_blank">Matsuoka et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.2967/jnumed.118.208009" target="_blank">Tyacke et al. (2018)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41380-021-01429-y" target="_blank">Livingston et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41380-025-03355-9" target="_blank">Best et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aax5440" target="_blank">Fultz et al. (2019)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2025.121142" target="_blank">Kim, Huang, &amp; Liu (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-37685-5" target="_blank">Eide et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02073-3" target="_blank">Hirschler et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-68374-8" target="_blank">Dagum et al. (2026)</a></td>
<td>It becomes much clearer which <strong>living-human in vivo</strong> advances are regional synaptic-density PET, <strong>1H-MRSI parcel similarity</strong>, <strong>high-resolution 1H-MRSI metabolite distribution</strong>, <strong>31P metabolite / pH balance</strong>, <strong>31P MT exchange-flux</strong>, <strong>31P NAD-content mapping</strong>, <strong>localized functional 31P NAD-dynamics</strong>, <strong>deuterium metabolite-mapping / absolute quantification</strong>, <strong>deuterium kinetic-rate imaging</strong>, <strong>tract-speed plus quantity-defined myelin / oligodendrocyte proxy routes with an internal family split</strong>, <strong>BBB water-exchange MRI</strong>, <strong>tracer-specific BBB PET transport</strong>, <strong>target-defined astrocyte PET with SMBT-1 target-validation / AD-context / brain-quantification / whole-body-biodistribution, SL25.1188 simplified-quantification / severity-conditioned MAO-B, and I2BS slices</strong>, <strong>macroscopic CSF oscillation</strong>, <strong>parenchyma-CSF water exchange</strong>, <strong>intrathecal tracer retention / CSF-to-blood clearance</strong>, <strong>human CSF-mobility MRI</strong>, and <strong>model-based overnight biomarker efflux</strong>. Just as importantly, these papers differ in <strong>safe calibrator role</strong>: the route can calibrate regional synaptic-density contrast, parcel-level metabolite similarity, high-resolution metabolite-distribution structure, bounded energetic balance, model-conditioned exchange flux, macro NAD-content maps, localized NAD dynamics, deuterium absolute metabolite mapping / quantification, kinetic-rate maps, tract-speed or bounded macro myelin / oligodendrocyte-linked proxy classes, target-defined astrocyte-related burden under a named tracer family and quantification regime, sleep-state CSF oscillation, parenchyma-CSF water exchange, intrathecal-tracer / CSF-to-blood-clearance capacity, CSF mobility, or model-based biomarker efflux without becoming one common state meter.</td>
<td>These papers do not provide a comparable in vivo whole-brain readout of current transcription / chromatin state, current post-transcriptional RNA-state, current phospho-signaling / second-messenger state, ECM / PNN gate state, branch-local proteostasis / synaptic-tag capture, branch- or bouton-specific cargo-routing, chloride homeostasis, branch-local mitochondrial positioning, cell-specific immune-controller state, or cell-specific neurovascular-controller state, and they do not yet compose into same-subject, same-session, externally calibrated state closure. A proxy-rich bundle still needs explicit calibrator-role logging and shared-driver audit.</td>
<td><a href="wbe_101.html#human-observability-ladder">WBE 101: human observability ladder</a> / <a href="wiki/measurement-stack-and-claim-ceiling.html#human-maintenance-proxy-ladder">Wiki: human maintenance-state proxy ladder</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>state-continuity bridge / sequential same-brain claims</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.crmeth.2023.100520" target="_blank">Lu et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-022-30199-6" target="_blank">Bosch et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08790-w" target="_blank">MICrONS Consortium et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-70609-x" target="_blank">Egger et al. (2024)</a></td>
<td>Same-subject or same-brain wording can now be read more narrowly: it may justify specimen linkage, landmark-based correlative workflow, or cross-day reacquisition design, but only after <strong>acquisition order</strong>, <strong>elapsed time</strong>, <strong>regime continuity</strong>, and <strong>bridge-validation rung</strong> are disclosed.</td>
<td>Do not read these papers as same-time capture, same-state continuity, or maintenance-consistent multistack closure.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/state-continuity-bridge.html">Wiki: State-Continuity Bridge</a> / <a href="verification.html#state-continuity-bridge-card">Verification: State-Continuity Bridge Card</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>neurovascular / BBB / blood-CSF barrier route-card split</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2010.09.043" target="_blank">Bell et al. (2010)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.3389/fncel.2020.00027" target="_blank">Kisler et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2023.08.030" target="_blank">Pandey et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.89611" target="_blank">Swissa et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-61312-0" target="_blank">Mai-Morente et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neurobiolaging.2024.12.012" target="_blank">Padrela et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-58356-7" target="_blank">Chung et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1186/s12987-020-00218-z" target="_blank">Zhao et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118755" target="_blank">Petitclerc et al. (2021)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1080/21688370.2021.1963143" target="_blank">Anderson et al. (2022)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X251369218" target="_blank">Wu et al. (2026)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1177/0271678X261429042" target="_blank">Petitclerc et al. (2026)</a></td>
<td>It becomes concrete that <strong>pericyte controller causality</strong>, <strong>activity-linked BBB modulation</strong>, <strong>capillary-diameter support for memory</strong>, <strong>human BBB permeability / exchange imaging</strong>, and <strong>human blood-CSF barrier / choroid-plexus transport imaging</strong> are related but non-identical route families. Controller-side biology, BBB imaging, and BCSFB imaging are no longer safe to collapse into one vascular bucket.</td>
<td>These papers do not provide a direct human readout of cell-specific neurovascular-controller state, choroid-plexus epithelial transporter identity, synapse-resolved support control, or local barrier-maintenance closure, and the human routes still remain macro permeability / exchange proxies rather than local controller measurements.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/homeostatic-plasticity-and-maintenance-state.html#neurovascular-unit-bbb-and-pericyte-state">Wiki: neurovascular-unit / BBB / pericyte state</a> / <a href="wbe_101.html#human-observability-ladder">WBE 101: human observability ladder</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>shared extracellular / electrical-state route split</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/47029" target="_blank">Galarreta &amp; Hestrin (1999)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.2727" target="_blank">Anastassiou et al. (2011)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.4493-13.2014" target="_blank">Graydon et al. (2014)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/jnr.20871" target="_blank">Kilb et al. (2006)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1241224" target="_blank">Xie et al. (2013)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroimage.2020.116682" target="_blank">Voldsbekk et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/ejn.70401" target="_blank">Feld et al. (2026)</a></td>
<td>It becomes concrete that <strong>gap-junction coupling</strong>, <strong>endogenous-field / ephaptic effects</strong>, <strong>extracellular-space geometry / diffusion barriers / osmotic regime</strong>, <strong>sleep-linked interstitial-space shifts</strong>, and <strong>bounded human diffusion-MRI or perturbation clues</strong> are related but non-identical route families. Fast synchrony, spillover control, and state-transition evidence are no longer safe to compress into one electrical-state bucket.</td>
<td>These papers do not provide one direct whole-brain readout of the current shared extracellular / electrical state, local inhibitory driving force, or cell-specific extracellular geometry in vivo, and the current human routes remain bounded proxy or perturbation clues rather than local ground truth.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/connectome-is-not-enough.html#electrical-state-route-card">Wiki: shared extracellular / electrical-state route card</a> / <a href="verification.html#observability-budget">Verification: Observability Budget</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>ECM / PNN route-family split</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1072699" target="_blank">Pizzorusso et al. (2002)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.2338" target="_blank">Frischknecht et al. (2009)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cell.2020.05.050" target="_blank">Nguyen et al. (2020)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matbio.2024.11.001" target="_blank">Jabłońska et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1626-24.2024" target="_blank">Alexander et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/acel.70139" target="_blank">Mehak et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1002/epi4.12963" target="_blank">Lehner et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s12035-024-04306-1" target="_blank">Banovac et al. (2025)</a></td>
<td>It becomes concrete that <strong>plasticity-window reopening</strong>, <strong>receptor-mobility / short-term-plasticity constraint</strong>, <strong>microglia-driven ECM remodeling</strong>, <strong>synapse-specific inhibitory-plasticity control</strong>, <strong>CA2-versus-PV memory support</strong>, <strong>age-linked rescue</strong>, and <strong>human ex vivo histology</strong> are related but non-identical route families. Matrix evidence is no longer safe to compress into one support or one plasticity bucket.</td>
<td>These papers do not provide a direct living-human whole-brain readout of current ECM / PNN gate state, one common plasticity meter, or a solved human in vivo maintenance-state observable.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/homeostatic-plasticity-and-maintenance-state.html#ecm-pnn-route-card">Wiki: ECM / PNN route card</a> / <a href="verification.html#maintenance-state-error-budget">Verification: Maintenance-State Error Budget</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>phospho-signaling / second-messenger route-card split</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/S0092-8674(03)00122-3" target="_blank">Lee et al. (2003)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.5303-03.2004" target="_blank">Rodrigues et al. (2004)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.0248-16.2016" target="_blank">Havekes et al. (2016)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-023-40930-6" target="_blank">Vierra et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adj1010" target="_blank">Barone et al. (2023)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biopsych.2023.12.020" target="_blank">Altas et al. (2024)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1619-24.2025" target="_blank">Rodriguez et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jproteome.2c00244" target="_blank">Biswas et al. (2023)</a></td>
<td>It becomes concrete that phospho papers split into <strong>phosphosite-specific plasticity gates</strong>, <strong>learning-linked local phosphorylation</strong>, <strong>compartmentalized second-messenger / signalosome routing</strong>, <strong>circadian phospho-timing gates</strong>, <strong>region-specific synapse relocalization</strong>, <strong>single-site phospho-mutant causal interventions</strong>, and <strong>human ex vivo phosphoproteome atlases</strong>. These are related but non-identical route families with different direct observables and different claim ceilings.</td>
<td>These papers do not provide a direct living-human whole-brain readout of current phosphosite occupancy, kinase/phosphatase balance, or compartment-specific second-messenger nanodomains, and they do not close one universal memory controller or current whole-brain phospho-state.</td>
<td><a href="wiki/homeostatic-plasticity-and-maintenance-state.html#phospho-route-card">Wiki: phospho-signaling route card</a> / <a href="wbe_101.html#state-completeness">WBE 101: state completeness</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>maintenance-state / mechanistic boundary</strong></td>
<td><a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-66975-3" target="_blank">Hadzibegovic et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-02152-5" target="_blank">Benoit et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cell.2016.01.046" target="_blank">Hengen et al. (2016)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-025-21967-7" target="_blank">Tallman et al. (2025)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1093/cercor/bhs014" target="_blank">Huber et al. (2013)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/ncomms12455" target="_blank">Kuhn et al. (2016)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brs.2017.11.016" target="_blank">Zrenner et al. (2018)</a>, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brs.2024.12.1193" target="_blank">Khatri et al.
